and thus, we get that
\Gamma_{i}\in\mathbb{Z}
.

Therefore
\mathfrak{q}_{1}(z)
and
\mathfrak{a}_{1}(z)
belong to
L_{1}(z)
. Now, we prove that
\mathfrak{q}_{1}(z)
belongs to
\mathcal{O}_{L_{1}}[[z]]
and that it is a unit of this ring. Indeed, we have already seen that
|\alpha_{c_{i}}|=1
for any
1\leq i\leq m
and therefore,
|\mathfrak{q}_{1}(z)|_{\mathcal{G}}=1=|\mathfrak{q}_{1}(0)|
. Further, we have
\mathfrak{q}_{1}(z)\in E(D_{0})
because
|\alpha_{c_{i}}|=1
for all
1\leq i\leq m
. So, Remark
5.2
implies that
\mathfrak{q}_{1}(z)\in\mathcal{O}_{L_{1}}[[z]]
and given that
|\mathfrak{q}_{1}(0)|=1
then
\mathfrak{q}_{1}(z)
is a unit of
\mathcal{O}_{L_{1}}[[z]]
. In a similar fashion, we prove that
\mathfrak{a}_{1}(z)
belongs to
\mathcal{O}_{L_{1}}[[z]]
and that it is a unit of this ring. We also have
h_{1}(z)\in 1+z\mathcal{O}_{L_{1}}[[z]]
because
f_{1}(z),\ldots,f_{m}(z)\in 1+z\mathcal{O}_{K}[[z]]
and for all
1\leq i\leq m
,
d_{i}\in\mathbb{Z}
and hence, for every
1\leq i\leq m
,
f_{i}(z)^{d_{i}}\in 1+z\mathcal{O}_{K}[[z]]
. Therefore, from Equation (
36
), we obtain

\mathfrak{j}_{1}(z)=\frac{h_{1}(z)}{\zeta\mathfrak{q}_{1}(z)\mathfrak{a}_{1}(z)}\in\mathcal{O}_{L_{1}}[[z]]\text{ and }|\mathfrak{j}_{1}(z)|_{\mathcal{G}}=1=|\mathfrak{j}_{1}(0)|.

Whence,
\mathfrak{j}_{1}(z)
is a unit of
\mathcal{O}_{L_{1}}[[z]]
.

(ii) It is clear, by construction, that
p
does not divide
d_{s}
.

(iii) Notice that
|\mathfrak{a}_{1}(z)|_{\mathcal{G}}=1
because for all
m+1\leq i\leq q
,
|\beta_{i}|=1
. Since the poles and zeros of
\mathfrak{a}_{1}(z)
are in
\{\beta_{m+1},\ldots,\beta_{q}\}
, we get that all the poles and zeros of
\mathfrak{a}_{1}(z)
have norm 1. Recall that we have assumed that, for all
1\leq i\leq m
,
D_{\alpha_{i}}=D_{\alpha_{c_{i}}}
. Further, for all
1\leq i,j\leq q
,
D_{\alpha_{i}}\neq D_{\alpha_{j}}
for
j\neq i
because
D_{\alpha_{1}},\ldots,D_{\alpha_{q}}
are the poles of
h^{\prime}(z)/h(z)
. In particular, Remark
6
implies that, for all
1\leq i\leq m
,
\beta_{m+1},\ldots,\beta_{q}
do not belong to
D_{\alpha_{c_{i}}}
. So, for every
1\leq i\leq m
,
\mathfrak{a}_{1}(z)\in E(D_{\alpha_{c_{i}}})
and
\mathfrak{a}_{1}(\alpha_{c_{i}})\neq 0
.

(iv) We have already seen that
|\mathfrak{j}_{1}(0)|=1
. We now prove that
\mathfrak{j}_{1}(z)=\mathfrak{j}_{1}(0)\bmod\pi_{L_{1}}\mathcal{O}_{L_{1}}[[z]]
. Let us write
\mathfrak{j}_{1}(z)=\sum_{j\geq 0}\kappa_{j}z^{j}
. It is clear that
\lambda\mathfrak{j}(z)=\mathfrak{j}_{1}(z)^{p^{s}}
and that
\mathfrak{j}_{1}(z)^{p^{s}}=\sum_{j\geq 0}\kappa_{j}^{p^{s}}z^{jp^{s}}\bmod\pi_{L_{1}}\mathcal{O}_{L_{1}}[[z]]
. But we have already proven that
\lambda\mathfrak{j}(z)=1\bmod\pi_{L_{1}}\mathcal{O}_{L_{1}}[[z]]
and thus
\kappa_{j}\in\pi_{L_{1}}\mathcal{O}_{L_{1}}
for all
j\geq 1
.

Finally, we proceed to prove that
\mathfrak{j}^{\prime}_{1}(z)\in\pi_{L}^{2}\mathcal{O}_{L}[[z]]
, where
L
is a finite extension of
L_{1}
. Since
\mathfrak{j}_{1}(z)=\mathfrak{j}_{1}(0)\bmod\pi_{L_{1}}\mathcal{O}_{L_{1}}[[z]]
, by reducing Equation (
36
) modulo
\pi_{L_{1}}
, we have

h_{1}(z)=\zeta\mathfrak{j}_{1}(0)\mathfrak{q}_{1}(z)\mathfrak{a}_{1}(z)\bmod\pi_{L_{1}}\mathcal{O}_{L_{1}}[[z]].

Notice that
\mathfrak{j}_{1}(0)
is a unit of
\mathcal{O}_{L_{1}}
because
|\mathfrak{j}_{1}(0)|=1
. In addition, we have already seen that
\mathfrak{q}_{1}(z)
and
\mathfrak{a}_{1}(z)
are units of
\mathcal{O}_{L_{1}}[[z]]
but
h_{1}(z)
is also a unit of this ring because
h_{1}(z)\in 1+z\mathcal{O}_{K}[[z]]
. So, from the equality
h_{1}(z)=\zeta\mathfrak{j}_{1}(0)\mathfrak{q}_{1}(z)\mathfrak{a}_{1}(z)\bmod\pi_{L_{1}}\mathcal{O}_{L_{1}}[[z]]
, we obtain

(37)

\frac{h_{1}^{\prime}(z)}{h_{1}(z)}=\frac{\mathfrak{q}_{1}^{\prime}(z)}{\mathfrak{q}_{1}(z)}+\frac{\mathfrak{a}^{\prime}_{1}(z)}{\mathfrak{a}_{1}(z)}\bmod\pi_{L_{1}}\mathcal{O}_{L_{1}}[[z]]

Let us show that
\frac{\mathfrak{j}^{\prime}_{1}(z)}{\mathfrak{j}_{1}(z)}
belongs to
E_{0,p}
. From Equation (
36
), we deduce that

\frac{\mathfrak{j}^{\prime}_{1}(z)}{\mathfrak{j}_{1}(z)}=\frac{h^{\prime}_{1}(z)}{h_{1}(z)}-\frac{\mathfrak{q}^{\prime}_{1}(z)}{\mathfrak{q}_{1}(z)}-\frac{\mathfrak{a}^{\prime}_{1}(z)}{\mathfrak{a}_{1}(z)}.

Given that
\frac{h^{\prime}_{1}(z)}{h_{1}(z)}=\sum_{i=1}^{m}d_{i}\frac{f^{\prime}_{i}(z)}{f_{i}(z)}
and
\frac{f^{\prime}_{i}(z)}{f_{i}(z)}\in E_{0,K}
for all
1\leq i\leq m
, we have
\frac{h^{\prime}_{1}(z)}{h_{1}(z)}\in E_{0,K}
. Further,
\frac{\mathfrak{q}^{\prime}_{1}(z)}{\mathfrak{q}_{1}(z)}=\sum_{i=1}^{m}\frac{\Gamma_{i}}{z-\alpha_{c_{i}}}
and since
|\alpha_{c_{i}}|=1
for all
1\leq i\leq m
, we get
\frac{\mathfrak{q}^{\prime}_{1}(z)}{\mathfrak{q}_{1}(z)}\in E_{0,p}
. In a similar way, we show that
\frac{\mathfrak{a}^{\prime}_{1}(z)}{\mathfrak{a}_{1}(z)}\in E_{0,p}
. Whence,
\mathfrak{j}^{\prime}_{1}(z)/\mathfrak{j}_{1}(z)
belongs to
E_{0,p}
.

Now, as
\mathfrak{j}_{1}(z)\in\mathcal{O}_{L_{1}}[[z]]
and
\mathfrak{j}^{\prime}_{1}(z)/\mathfrak{j}_{1}(z)\in E_{0,p}
, it follows from Theorem 3.1 of
[
24
]
that there exits
R_{2}(z)\in\mathbb{C}_{p}(z)
such that
R_{2}(z)
has no poles or zeros in
D_{0}
and

(38)

\left|\frac{\mathfrak{j}^{\prime}_{1}(z)}{\mathfrak{j}_{1}(z)}-\frac{R^{\prime}_{2}(z)}{R_{2}(z)}\right|_{\mathcal{G}}<1/p^{2}.

In consequence

(39)

\frac{h_{1}^{\prime}(z)}{h_{1}(z)}=\frac{\mathfrak{q}_{1}^{\prime}(z)}{\mathfrak{q}_{1}(z)}+\frac{\mathfrak{a}^{\prime}_{1}(z)}{\mathfrak{a}_{1}(z)}+\frac{R^{\prime}_{2}(z)}{R_{2}(z)}+g_{2}

with
g_{2}\in E_{0,p}
and
|g_{2}|_{\mathcal{G}}<1/p^{2}
.

Let
L
be the extension of
L_{1}
obtained by adding the zeros and poles of
R_{2}(z)
. Let us show that
R^{\prime}_{2}(z)/R_{2}(z)
belongs to
\mathcal{O}_{L}[[z]]
. It is clear that
R^{\prime}_{2}(z)/R_{2}(z)\in L(z)
. Further,
\mathfrak{j}_{1}^{\prime}(z)/\mathfrak{j}_{1}(z)\in\mathcal{O}_{L}[[z]]
because we know that
\mathfrak{j}_{1}(z)
is a unit of the ring
\mathcal{O}_{L_{1}}[[z]]
. So, we deduce, from Equation (
38
), that

\left|\frac{R^{\prime}_{2}(z)}{R_{2}(z)}\right|_{\mathcal{G}}=\left|\frac{R^{\prime}_{2}(z)}{R_{2}(z)}-\frac{\mathfrak{j}^{\prime}_{1}(z)}{\mathfrak{j}_{1}(z)}+\frac{\mathfrak{j}^{\prime}_{1}(z)}{\mathfrak{j}_{1}(z)}\right|_{\mathcal{G}}\leq\max\left\{\left|\frac{\mathfrak{j}^{\prime}_{1}(z)}{\mathfrak{j}_{1}(z)}-\frac{R^{\prime}_{2}(z)}{R_{2}(z)}\right|_{\mathcal{G}},\left|\frac{\mathfrak{j}^{\prime}_{1}(z)}{\mathfrak{j}_{1}(z)}\right|_{\mathcal{G}}\right\}\leq 1.

Further,
R^{\prime}_{2}(z)/R_{2}(z)
belongs to
E(D_{0})
because
D_{0}
is not a pole of
h^{\prime}_{1}(z)/h_{1}(z)
(recall that
h^{\prime}_{1}(z)/h_{1}(z)\in E_{0,K})
. Then, by Remark
5.2
,
R^{\prime}_{2}(z)/R_{2}(z)
belongs
\mathcal{O}_{L}[[z]]
.

Consequently, it follows from Equation (
39
) that
g_{2}\in\mathcal{O}_{L}[[z]]
and since
|g_{2}|_{\mathcal{G}}<1/p^{2}
,
g_{2}\in\pi^{2}_{L}\mathcal{O}_{L}[[z]]
. So, reducing Equation (
39
) modulo
\pi^{2}_{L}
, we have

(40)

\frac{h_{1}^{\prime}(z)}{h_{1}(z)}=\frac{\mathfrak{q}_{1}^{\prime}(z)}{\mathfrak{q}_{1}(z)}+\frac{\mathfrak{a}^{\prime}_{1}(z)}{\mathfrak{a}_{1}(z)}+\frac{R^{\prime}_{2}(z)}{R_{2}(z)}\bmod\pi_{L}^{2}\mathcal{O}_{L}[[z]].

From Equation (
37
), we know that
\frac{h_{1}^{\prime}(z)}{h_{1}(z)}=\frac{\mathfrak{q}_{1}^{\prime}(z)}{\mathfrak{q}_{1}(z)}+\frac{\mathfrak{a}^{\prime}_{1}(z)}{\mathfrak{a}_{1}(z)}\bmod\pi_{L}\mathcal{O}_{L}[[z]]
and therefore, from Equation (
40
), we have
\frac{R^{\prime}_{2}(z)}{R_{2}(z)}\in\pi_{L}\mathcal{O}_{L}[[z]]
.

Let us write

\frac{R^{\prime}_{2}(z)}{R_{2}(z)}=\sum_{\tau\in S_{1}}\frac{c_{\tau}}{z-\tau},

where
S_{1}
is the set of poles and zero of
R_{2}(z)
and
c_{\tau}\in\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{0\}
.

So, for all
\tau\in S_{1}
,
D_{\tau}\in\{D_{\alpha_{1}},\ldots,D_{\alpha_{q}}\}
. Given that,
\frac{\mathfrak{q}^{\prime}_{1}(z)}{\mathfrak{q}_{1}(z)}=\sum_{i=1}^{m}\frac{\Gamma_{i}}{z-\alpha_{c_{i}}}
and
\frac{\mathfrak{a}^{\prime}_{1}(z)}{\mathfrak{a}_{1}(z)}=\sum_{i=m+1}^{q}\frac{\Gamma_{i}}{z-\beta_{i}}
then, by using (ii) of Lemma
6.0.1
, we deduce from Equation (
40
) that, for every
1\leq i\leq q
,

(41)

res\left(\frac{h^{\prime}_{1}(z)}{h_{1}(z)},D_{\alpha_{i}}\right)=(\Gamma_{i}+\sum_{\tau\in S_{1}\cap D_{\alpha_{i}}}c_{\tau})\bmod p^{2}.

But,
\Gamma_{i}=res\left(\frac{h^{\prime}_{1}(z)}{h_{1}(z)},D_{\alpha_{i}}\right)
.
Whence,

(42)

\sum_{\tau\in S_{1}\cap D_{\alpha_{i}}}c_{\tau}=0\bmod p^{2}\mathbb{Z}.

Further, if
\tau\in D_{\alpha_{i}}\cap S_{1}
, by Lemma
6.0.1
, we conclude that

\frac{1}{z-\tau}=\frac{1}{z-\alpha_{i}}\sum_{l\geq 0}\left(\frac{\tau-\alpha_{i}}{z-\alpha_{i}}\right)^{l}.

Since
|\alpha_{i}-\tau|<1
, we have
\alpha_{i}-\tau\in\pi_{L}\mathcal{O}_{L}
. So, from the previous equality, we get

\frac{1}{z-\tau}=\frac{1}{z-\alpha_{i}}+\frac{\tau-\alpha_{i}}{(z-\alpha_{i})^{2}}\bmod\pi_{L}^{2}\mathcal{O}_{L}[[z]].

But
c_{\tau}\in\pi_{L}\mathcal{O}_{L}
because
\frac{R^{\prime}_{2}(z)}{R_{2}(z)}\in\pi_{L}\mathcal{O}_{L}[[z]].
So, from the previous equality, we get

(43)

\frac{c_{\tau}}{z-\tau}=\frac{c_{\tau}}{z-\alpha_{i}}\bmod\pi_{L}^{2}\mathcal{O}_{L}[[z]].

Therefore, combining Equations (
40
), (
42
), and (
43
), we deduce that

\frac{h_{1}^{\prime}(z)}{h_{1}(z)}=\sum_{i=0}^{m}\frac{\Gamma_{i}+\sum_{\tau\in S\cap D_{\alpha_{i}}}c_{\tau}}{z-\alpha_{c_{i}}}+\sum_{i=m+1}^{q}\frac{\Gamma_{i}+\sum_{\tau\in S\cap D_{\alpha_{i}}}c_{\tau}}{z-\beta_{i}}=\sum_{i=0}^{m}\frac{\Gamma_{i}}{z-\alpha_{c_{i}}}+\sum_{i=m+1}^{q}\frac{\Gamma_{i}}{z-\beta_{i}}\bmod\pi_{L}^{2}\mathcal{O}_{L}[[z]].

It follows from Equation (
36
) that

\frac{h_{1}^{\prime}(z)}{h_{1}(z)}=\sum_{i=0}^{m}\frac{\Gamma_{i}}{z-\alpha_{c_{i}}}+\sum_{i=m+1}^{q}\frac{\Gamma_{i}}{z-\beta_{i}}+\frac{\mathfrak{j}_{1}^{\prime}(z)}{\mathfrak{j}_{1}(z)}.

So, from the previous two equalities we get

\frac{\mathfrak{j}_{1}^{\prime}(z)}{\mathfrak{j}_{1}(z)}\in\pi_{L}^{2}\mathcal{O}_{L}[[z]].

We have already seen that
\mathfrak{j}_{1}(z)
is a unit of
\mathcal{O}_{L}[[z]]
and therefore,
\mathfrak{j}^{\prime}_{1}(z)\in\pi_{L}^{2}\mathcal{O}_{L}[[z]]
.

(v) Since, for all
1\leq i\leq r
,
p^{s}d_{i}=a_{i}
, we have
(f^{d_{1}}_{1}(z)\cdots f^{d_{r}}_{r}(z))^{p^{s}}=f^{a_{1}}_{1}(z)\cdots f^{a_{r}}_{r}(z)
. But, we know that
f^{a_{1}}_{1}(z)\cdots f^{a_{r}}_{r}(z)\in E_{0,K}
.

6.3.
Proof of Theorems
2.4
and
2.4

As illustration of Theorem
6.1
, we prove Theorems
2.4
and
2.4
. The proof of both theorems runs along the same lines, we only need to verify that the assumptions of Theorem
6.1
are satisfied.

Proof 6.8

(Proof of Theorem
2.4
)
.

The power series
\mathfrak{h}(z)
,
\mathfrak{f}(z)
and
\mathfrak{g}(z)
are respectively solution of the following hypergeometric operators

\mathcal{L}:=\delta^{3}-z(\delta+1/2)^{3},\quad\delta^{3}-z(\delta-1/2)(\delta+1/2)^{2},\quad\mathcal{D}:=\delta^{2}-z(\delta+1/2)^{2}.

It is clear that these differential operators are MOM at zero and, it follows from
[
28
, Theorem 6.2]
that they have strong Frobenius structure for
p=3
. For this reason
\mathfrak{h}(z)
,
\mathfrak{f}(z)
,
\mathfrak{g}(z)
belong to
\mathcal{M}\mathcal{F}(\mathbb{Q}_{3})
. Recall that
\mathbb{Q}_{3}
is a Frobenius field. In addition, since
K
is an extension of
\mathbb{Q}_{3}
, we have
\mathcal{M}\mathcal{F}(\mathbb{Q}_{3})\subset\mathcal{M}\mathcal{F}(K)
. So
\mathfrak{h}(z)
,
\mathfrak{f}(z)
,
\mathfrak{g}(z)
belong to
\mathcal{M}\mathcal{F}(K)
.

(i). We first show that
\mathfrak{f}(z)/\mathfrak{h}(z)
belongs to
E_{\mathbb{Q}_{3}}
. By
[
28
, Theorem 6.2]
again, we know that
\delta^{3}-z(\delta+1/2)^{3}
has a strong Frobenius structure for
p=3
of period
1
. Thus, according to (ii) of Theorem
3
, we get that
\mathfrak{h}(z)/\mathfrak{h}(z^{3})\in E_{\mathbb{Q}_{3}}
. But, according to Corollary 2 of Section 1 of
[
14
]
and Theorem 2 of
[
14
]
,
\mathfrak{f}(z)/\mathfrak{h}(z^{3})\in E_{\mathbb{Q}_{3}}
. Given that
E_{\mathbb{Q}_{3}}
is a field we obtain
\mathfrak{f}(z)/\mathfrak{h}(z)\in E_{\mathbb{Q}_{3}}
.

Finally, as
\mathfrak{h}(z)
and
\mathfrak{f}(z)
belong to
\mathcal{M}\mathcal{F}(\mathbb{Q}_{3})
and
\mathbb{Q}_{3}
is a Frobenius field then, from Lemma
3
, we deduce that
\mathfrak{f}^{(s)}(z)/\mathfrak{f}(z)
and
\mathfrak{h}(z)/\mathfrak{h}^{(r)}(z)
belong to
E_{\mathbb{Q}_{3}}
for all integers
r,s\geq 0
. Consequently,

\frac{\mathfrak{f}^{(s)}(z)}{\mathfrak{h}^{(r)}(z)}=\frac{\mathfrak{f}^{(s)}(z)}{\mathfrak{f}(z)}\cdot\frac{\mathfrak{f}(z)}{\mathfrak{h}(z)}\cdot\frac{\mathfrak{h}(z)}{\mathfrak{h}^{(r)}(z)}

belongs to
E_{\mathbb{Q}_{3}}
for all integers
r,s\geq 0
.

Given that
K
is an extension of
\mathbb{Q}_{3}
, we get
E_{\mathbb{Q}_{3}}\subset E_{K}
and thus, for all integers
r,s\geq 0
,
\frac{\mathfrak{f}^{(s)}(z)}{\mathfrak{h}^{(r)}(z)}
belongs to
E_{K}
.

(ii). In order to prove that, for all integers
r,s\geq 0
,
\mathfrak{h}^{(r)}(z)
and
\mathfrak{g}^{(s)}(z)
are algebraically independent over
E_{K}
, it sufficient, according to Theorem
2.3
, to show that
\mathfrak{h}(z)
and
\mathfrak{g}(z)
are algebraically independent over
E_{K}
. Our strategy is to suppose that
\mathfrak{h}(z)
and
\mathfrak{g}(z)
are algebraically dependent over
E_{K}
and then, by applying Theorem
6.1
, we get a contradiction. For this purpose, we split our argument into several steps.

First Step
We show that
\mathfrak{h}^{\prime}(z)/\mathfrak{h}(z)\in E(\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{C}_{3}}\setminus D_{\alpha_{1}})
and that
\mathfrak{g}^{\prime}(z)/\mathfrak{g}\in E(\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{C}_{3}}\setminus D_{\alpha_{2}})
with
\alpha_{1}=1
and
\alpha_{2}=-1
.

It follows from Corollary 1 of Section 1 of
[
14
]
and Theorem 2 of
[
14
]
that, for all integers
r\geq 1
,

(44)

\frac{\mathfrak{h}(z)}{\mathfrak{h}(z^{3})}=\frac{\mathfrak{h}_{r}(z)}{\mathfrak{h}_{r-1}(z^{3})}\bmod 3^{r}\mathbb{Z}[[z]],

where, for
r\geq 0
,
\mathfrak{h}_{r}(z)
is the
3^{r}-1
-th truncation of
\mathfrak{h}(z)
. Thus, according to Lemma 2 of
[
2
]
,
\mathfrak{h}(z)/\mathfrak{h}(z^{3})
extends to
\Omega=\{x\in\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{C}_{3}}:|\mathfrak{h}_{1}(x)|=1\}
. As
\mathfrak{h}_{1}(z)=1+\frac{1}{8}z+\frac{27}{512}z^{2}
then, it is not hard to see that,
|\mathfrak{h}_{1}(x)|<1
if and only if
x\in D_{\alpha_{1}}
. So,
\Omega=\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{C}_{3}}\setminus D_{\alpha_{1}}
. We put
H(z)=\mathfrak{h}(z)/\mathfrak{h}(z^{3})
. Then
H\in E(\Omega)
. Moreover, from (iii) of Lemma 2 of
[
2
]
, we also have
|H(x)|=1
for all
x\in\Omega
. Hence,
H
is a unit of the ring
E(\Omega)
. It is not hard to see that

H^{\prime}(z)=H(z)\left[\frac{\mathfrak{h}^{\prime}(z)}{\mathfrak{h}(z)}-3z^{2}\frac{\mathfrak{h}^{\prime}(z^{3)}}{\mathfrak{h}(z^{3})}\right].

Therefore, if for every
m\geq 0
, we consider
H_{m}=H(z)H(z^{p})\cdots H(z^{p^{m}})
then
H_{m}\in E(\Omega)
and
|H_{m}(x)|=1
for all
x\in\Omega
. So
H_{m}
is a unit of
E(\Omega)
and we also have the equality

H^{\prime}_{m}(z)=H_{m}(z)\left[\frac{\mathfrak{h}^{\prime}(z)}{\mathfrak{h}(z)}-3^{m+1}z^{3^{m+1}-1}\frac{\mathfrak{h}^{\prime}(z^{3^{m+1}})}{\mathfrak{h}(z^{3^{m+1}})}\right].

Therefore, for all integers
m\geq 0
,

\frac{\mathfrak{h}^{\prime}(z)}{\mathfrak{h}(z)}\equiv\frac{H_{m}^{\prime}(z)}{H_{m}(z)}\bmod 3^{m+1}.

Since, for all
m\geq 0
,
H_{m}
is a unit of
E(\Omega)
, from the previous congruences, we conclude that
\frac{\mathfrak{h}^{\prime}(z)}{\mathfrak{h}(z)}
belongs to
E(\Omega)
.

We have a similar situation for
\mathfrak{g}
. It follows from Corollary 1 of Section 1 of
[
14
]
and Theorem 2 of
[
14
]
that, for all integers
r\geq 1
,

(45)

\frac{\mathfrak{g}(z)}{\mathfrak{g}(z^{3})}=\frac{\mathfrak{g}_{r}(z)}{\mathfrak{g}_{r-1}(z^{3})}\bmod 3^{r}\mathbb{Z}[[z]],

where, for
r\geq 0
,
\mathfrak{g}_{r}(z)
is the
3^{r}-1
-th truncation of
\mathfrak{h}(z)
. Thus, according to Lemma 2 of
[
2
]
,
\mathfrak{g}(z)/\mathfrak{g}(z^{3})
extends to
\Delta=\{x\in\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{C}_{3}}:|\mathfrak{g}_{1}(x)|=1\}
. As
\mathfrak{g}_{1}(z)=1+\frac{1}{4}z+\frac{9}{64}z^{2}
then, it is not hard to see that,
|\mathfrak{g}_{1}(x)|<1
if and only if
x\in D_{\alpha_{2}}
. So,
\Delta=\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{C}_{3}}\setminus D_{\alpha_{2}}
. We put
G(z)=\mathfrak{g}(z)/\mathfrak{g}(z^{3})
. Then
G\in E(\Delta)
. Moreover, from (iii) of Lemma 2 of
[
2
]
, we also have
|G(x)|=1
for all
x\in\Delta
. Hence,
G
is a unit of the ring
E(\Delta)
. So,
G_{m}=G(z)G(z^{p})\cdots G(z^{p^{m}})
is a unit of
G_{m}\in E(\Delta)
and
|G_{m}(x)|=1
for all
x\in\Delta
. By proceeding as before, we conclude that, for all integers
m\geq 0
,

\frac{\mathfrak{g}^{\prime}(z)}{\mathfrak{g}(z)}\equiv\frac{G_{m}^{\prime}(z)}{G_{m}(z)}\bmod 3^{m+1}.

Since, for all
m\geq 0
,
G_{m}
is a unit of
E(\Delta)
, from the previous congruences, we conclude that
\frac{\mathfrak{g}^{\prime}(z)}{\mathfrak{g}(z)}
belongs to
E(\Delta)
.

Second Step
We show that
D_{\alpha_{1}}
is a pole of o of
\mathfrak{h}^{\prime}/\mathfrak{h}
and that
D_{\alpha_{2}}
is a pole of
\mathfrak{g}^{\prime}/\mathfrak{g}
. It follows from Equations (
44
) and (
45
) that

\mathfrak{h}(z)=\mathfrak{h}_{1}(z)\mathfrak{h}(z^{3})\bmod 3,\quad\mathfrak{g}(z)=\mathfrak{g}_{1}(z)\mathfrak{g}(z^{3}).

For this reason

(46)

\frac{\mathfrak{h}^{\prime}(z)}{\mathfrak{h}(z)}=\frac{1}{z-1}\bmod 3,\quad\frac{\mathfrak{g}^{\prime}(z)}{\mathfrak{g}(z)}=\frac{1}{1+z}\bmod 3.

So
D_{\alpha_{1}}
is a pole of
\frac{\mathfrak{h}^{\prime}(z)}{\mathfrak{h}(z)}
and
D_{\alpha_{2}}
is a pole of
\frac{\mathfrak{g}^{\prime}(z)}{\mathfrak{g}(z)}
.

We now put

\lambda_{1}=res\left(\frac{\mathfrak{h}^{\prime}(z)}{\mathfrak{h}(z)},D_{\alpha_{1}}\right)\text{ and }\lambda_{2}=res\left(\frac{\mathfrak{g}^{\prime}(z)}{\mathfrak{g}(z)},D_{\alpha_{2}}\right).

Third Step
We now show that
\lambda_{1}\bmod 9=4=\lambda_{2}\bmod 9.

We deduce from Equation (
44
) that

\frac{\mathfrak{h}(z)}{\mathfrak{h}(z^{9})}=\frac{\mathfrak{h}_{2}(z)}{\mathfrak{h}_{1}(z^{3})}\frac{\mathfrak{h}_{2}(z^{3})}{\mathfrak{h}_{1}(z^{9})}\bmod 9.

Therefore,

\frac{\mathfrak{h}^{\prime}(z)}{\mathfrak{h}(z)}=\frac{8+8z+5z^{2}}{1-z^{3}}=\frac{2z+1}{z^{2}+z+1}-\frac{7}{z-1}\bmod 9.

Let
z_{0},z_{1}\in\overline{\mathbb{Q}_{3}}
be the roots of
1+z+z^{2}
. Then
|z_{0}|=1=|z_{1}|
,
z_{0}\neq z_{1}
, and
z_{0},z_{1},\in D_{\alpha_{1}}
because
1+z+z^{2}=(1-z)^{2}\bmod 3
. Furthermore, it is clear that

\frac{2z+1}{z^{2}+z+1}-\frac{7}{z-1}=\frac{1}{z-z_{0}}+\frac{1}{z-z_{1}}-\frac{7}{z-1}.

Since
1,z_{0},z_{1}\in D_{\alpha_{1}}
, it follows from (1) of Remark
6.0.1
and (ii) of Lemma
6.0.1
that

\lambda_{1}\bmod 9=(1+1-7)\bmod 9=-5\bmod 9=4.

Now, from Equation (
45
), we deduce that

\frac{\mathfrak{g}(z)}{\mathfrak{g}(z^{9})}=\frac{\mathfrak{g}_{2}(z)}{\mathfrak{g}_{1}(z^{3})}\frac{\mathfrak{g}_{2}(z^{3})}{\mathfrak{g}_{1}(z^{9})}\bmod 9.

Therefore,

\frac{\mathfrak{g}^{\prime}(z)}{\mathfrak{g}(z)}=\frac{-7-5z+3z^{2}+4z^{3}+8x^{4}-7z^{6}-5z^{7}}{(z-1)(z+1)(z^{6}-z^{3}+1)}\bmod 9.

Note that

\frac{-7-5z+3z^{2}+4z^{3}+8x^{4}-7z^{6}-5z^{7}}{(z-1)(z+1)(z^{6}-z^{3}+1)}=\frac{3-3z^{4}}{z^{6}-z^{3}+1}-\frac{1}{2(z+1)}\bmod 9.

Let
s
be the set of the roots of
z^{6}-z^{3}+1
. Since
z^{6}-z^{3}+1=(z+1)^{3}\bmod 3
, we have
s\subset D_{\alpha_{2}}
. Therefore, by (1) of Remark
6.0.1
and (ii) of Lemma
6.0.1
, we deduce that

\lambda_{2}\bmod 9=\left(\sum_{\tau\in\ s}res\left(\frac{3-3z^{4}}{z^{6}-z^{3}+1},\tau\right)-\frac{1}{2}\right)\bmod 9.

But
\sum_{\tau\in\ S}res\left(\frac{3-3z^{4}}{z^{6}-z^{3}+1},\tau\right)=0
and consequently
\lambda_{2}=4\bmod 9.

Fourth Step.
We now proceed to see that the conditions (1)-(2) of Theorem
6.1
are fulfilled. In First Step we proved that
\mathfrak{h}^{\prime}(z)/\mathfrak{h}(z)\in E(\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{C}_{3}}\setminus D_{\alpha_{1}})
with
\alpha_{1}=1
and that
\mathfrak{g}^{\prime}(z)/\mathfrak{g}(z)\in E(\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{C}_{3}}\setminus D_{\alpha_{2}})
with
\alpha_{2}=-1
. We also proved in Second Step that
D_{\alpha_{1}}
is a pole of
\mathfrak{h}^{\prime}(z)/\mathfrak{h}(z)
and that
D_{\alpha_{2}}
is a pole of
\mathfrak{g}^{\prime}(z)/\mathfrak{g}(z)
. Thus,
\textbf{Pol}(\mathfrak{h}^{\prime}(z)/\mathfrak{h}(z))=\{D_{\alpha_{1}}\}
and
\textbf{Pol}(\mathfrak{g}^{\prime}(z)/\mathfrak{g}(z))=\{D_{\alpha_{2}}\}
. In addition, notice that
D_{\alpha_{1}}\neq D_{\alpha 2}
because
|\alpha_{1}-\alpha_{2}|=1
.

Finally, we have
\mathcal{L}(\mathfrak{h})=0
and
\mathcal{D}(\mathfrak{g})=0
. Let us write
\mathcal{L}
and
\mathcal{D}
in terms of
d/dz
. Then,

\mathcal{L}_{1}=\frac{d}{dz^{3}}+\left(\frac{3}{z}-\frac{3}{2(1-z)}\right)\frac{d}{dz^{2}}+\left(\frac{1}{z^{2}}-\frac{3}{2z(1-z)}-\frac{1}{z(1-z)}\right)\frac{d}{dz}-\frac{1}{8z^{2}(z-1)}

\mathcal{D}_{1}=\frac{d}{dz^{2}}+\frac{1-2z}{z(z-1)}\frac{d}{dz}-\frac{1}{4z(z-1)}

So, it is clear that
\alpha_{1}
is a regular singular point of
\mathcal{L}_{1\mid\mathscr{M}^{2}}
and
\alpha_{2}
is also a regular singular point of
\mathcal{D}_{1\mid\mathscr{M}^{2}}
. The indicial polynomial of
\mathcal{L}_{1\mid\mathscr{M}^{2}}
at
\alpha_{1}
is the
P=X(X-1)(X-2)+\frac{3}{2}X(X-1)\in\frac{\mathbb{Z}}{9\mathbb{Z}}[X]
and the indicial polynomial of
\mathcal{D}_{1\mid\mathscr{M}^{2}}
at
\alpha_{2}
is
Q=X(X-1)(X-2)\in\frac{\mathbb{Z}}{9\mathbb{Z}}[X].

So, the conditions (1)-(2) of Theorem
6.1
are satisfied and, in oder to apply it, we suppose, towards a contradiction, that
\mathfrak{h}(z)
and
\mathfrak{g}(z)
are algebraically dependent over
E_{K}
and thus we have two cases.

case A:

res\left(\frac{\mathfrak{h}^{\prime}(z)}{\mathfrak{h}(z)},D_{\alpha_{1}}\right)
belongs to
\mathbb{Z}_{3}
and its reduction modulo
p^{2}
is an exponent of
\mathcal{L}_{1\mid\mathscr{M}^{2}}
at
\alpha_{1}
.

Or

case B:
es\left(\frac{\mathfrak{g}^{\prime}(z)}{\mathfrak{g}(z)},D_{\alpha_{2}}\right)
belongs to
\mathbb{Z}_{3}
and its reduction modulo
p^{2}
is an exponent of
\mathcal{D}_{1\mid\mathscr{M}^{2}}
at
\alpha_{2}
.

We are going to see that case A and B lead to a contradiction. According to Third Step,

res\left(\frac{\mathfrak{h}^{\prime}(z)}{\mathfrak{h}(z)},D_{\alpha_{1}}\right)\bmod 9=\lambda_{1}\bmod 9=4.

So case A implies that
4
is a solution of
P
. That is a contradiction because
P(4)=6
. We now consider case B. According to Third Step,

res\left(\frac{\mathfrak{g}^{\prime}(z)}{\mathfrak{g}(z)},D_{\alpha_{2}}\right)\bmod 9=\lambda_{2}\bmod 9=4.

Therefore,
4
is a solution of
Q
. Again, that is a contradiction because
Q(4)=6
.

(iii). Suppose that
\mathfrak{f}(z)
and
\mathfrak{g}(z)
are algebraically dependent over
E_{K}
. Then, by Theorem
2.3
, there are integers
a,b
not all zero such that
\mathfrak{f}^{a}(z)\mathfrak{g}^{b}(z)\in E_{K}.
According to (i),
\mathfrak{f}/\mathfrak{h}\in E_{K}
. Thus,

\mathfrak{h}^{a}(z)\mathfrak{g}^{b}(z)=\frac{\mathfrak{h}^{a}(z)}{\mathfrak{f}^{a}(z)}\mathfrak{f}^{a}(z)\mathfrak{g}^{b}(z)\in E_{K}.

Whence,
\mathfrak{g}(z)
and
\mathfrak{h}(z)
are algebraically dependent over
E_{K}
.That is a contradiction because, according to (ii),
\mathfrak{g}(z)
and
\mathfrak{h}(z)
are algebraically independent over
E_{K}
. Therefore,
\mathfrak{f}(z)
and
\mathfrak{g}(z)
are algebraically independent over
E_{K}
. Consequently, Theorem
2.3
implies that, for all integers
r,s\geq 0
,
\mathfrak{f}^{(r)}(z)
and
\mathfrak{g}^{(s)}(z)
are algebraically independent over
E_{K}
.

Proof 6.9

(Proof of Theorem
2.4
)
.

As we have already observed at the very beginning of the proof of Theorem
2.4
that
\mathfrak{h}(z)
belongs to
\mathcal{M}\mathcal{F}(K)
. We now prove that
\mathfrak{A}(z)\in\mathcal{M}\mathcal{F}(K)
. Since
\mathcal{M}\mathcal{F}(\mathbb{Q}_{3})\subset\mathcal{M}\mathcal{F}(K)
, it is sufficient to prove that
\mathfrak{A}(z)\in\mathcal{M}\mathcal{F}(\mathbb{Q}_{3})
. Indeed, by
[
2
, Theorem 1]
, we get that
\mathfrak{A}(z)/\mathfrak{A}(z^{3})
belongs to
E_{0,\mathbb{Q}_{3}}
. Consequently, from Remark
5.3
, we obtain
\mathfrak{A}^{\prime}(z)/\mathfrak{A}(z)
belongs to
E_{0,\mathbb{Q}_{3}}
. Further, it is clear that
\mathfrak{A}(z)
is solution of the differential operator
\delta-\frac{\delta\mathfrak{A}(z)}{\mathfrak{A}(z)}
. Notice that this differential operator is MOM at zero. In addition, this differential operator belongs to
E_{\mathbb{Q}_{3}}[\delta]
because
\mathfrak{A}^{\prime}(z)/\mathfrak{A}(z)\in E_{\mathbb{Q}_{3}}
and has a Frobenius structure because
\mathfrak{A}(z)/\mathfrak{A}(z^{3})\in E_{\mathbb{Q}_{3}}
. Finally
\left|\frac{\delta\mathfrak{A}(z)}{\mathfrak{A}(z)}\right|_{\mathcal{G}}\leq 1
because
\mathfrak{A}(z)\in 1+z\mathbb{Z}[[z]]
. So, we conclude that
\mathfrak{A}(z)\in\mathcal{M}\mathcal{F}(\mathbb{Q}_{3})
. Further, we also know that
\mathfrak{A}(z)
is solution of the differential operator

\mathcal{H}=\frac{d}{dz^{3}}+\frac{3-153z+6z^{2}}{(1-34z+z^{2})z}\frac{d}{dz^{2}}+\frac{1-112z+7z^{2}}{(1-34z+z^{2})z^{2}}\frac{d}{dz}+\frac{z-5}{(1-34z+z^{2})z^{2}}.

(i) According to Theorem
2.3
, in oder to prove that, for all integers
r,s\geq 0
,
\mathfrak{h}^{(r)}(z)
and
\mathfrak{A}^{(s)}(z)
are algebraically independent over
E_{K}
, it is sufficient to prove that
\mathfrak{h}(z)
and
\mathfrak{A}(z)
are algebraically independent over
E_{K}
. For this purpose, we suppose that
\mathfrak{h}(z)
and
\mathfrak{A}(z)
are algebraically dependent over
E_{K}
and then, by applying Theorem
6.1
, we get a contradiction. For this purpose, we split our argument into several steps.

First Step
We show that
\mathfrak{A}^{\prime}(z)/\mathfrak{A}(z)\in E(\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{C}_{3}}\setminus D_{\alpha_{2}})
with
\alpha_{2}=-1
. In fact, it follows from
[
2
, Theorem 1]
that, for all integers
r\geq 1
,

(47)

\frac{\mathfrak{A}(z)}{\mathfrak{A}(z^{3})}=\frac{\mathfrak{A}_{r}(z)}{\mathfrak{A}_{r-1}(z^{3})}\bmod 3^{r}\mathbb{Z}[[z]],

where for all
r\geq 0
,
\mathfrak{A}_{r}(z)
is the
3^{r}-1
-th truncation of
\mathfrak{A}(z)
. Thus, according to Lemma 2 of
[
2
]
,
\mathfrak{A}(z)/\mathfrak{A}(z^{3})
extends to
\Lambda=\{x\in\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{C}_{3}}:|\mathfrak{A}_{1}(x)|=1\}
. As
\mathfrak{A}_{1}(z)=1+5z+73z^{2}
then, it is not hard to see that,
|\mathfrak{A}_{1}(x)|<1
if and only if
x\in D_{\alpha_{2}}
. So,
\Lambda=\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{C}_{p}}\setminus D_{\alpha_{2}}
. So, proceeding in a similar fashion as in the First Step of the proof of Theorem
2.4
, we conclude that
\mathfrak{A}^{\prime}(z)/\mathfrak{A}(z)
belongs to
E(\Lambda)
.

Second Step
We now show that
D_{\alpha_{1}}
,
\alpha_{1}=1
, is a pole of
\mathfrak{h}^{\prime}/\mathfrak{h}
and that
D_{\alpha_{2}}
is a pole of
\mathfrak{A}^{\prime}/\mathfrak{A}
with
\alpha_{2}=-1
. From Second Step of the proof of Theorem
2.4
, we know that
D_{\alpha_{1}}
is a pole of
\mathfrak{h}^{\prime}(z)/\mathfrak{h}(z)
. Now, we deduce from Equation (
47
) that

\frac{\mathfrak{A}(z)}{\mathfrak{A}(z^{9})}=\frac{\mathfrak{A}_{2}(z)}{\mathfrak{A}_{1}(z^{3})}\frac{\mathfrak{A}_{2}(z^{3})}{\mathfrak{A}_{1}(z^{9})}\bmod 9\mathbb{Z}[[z]]

Therefore,

\frac{\mathfrak{A}^{\prime}(z)}{\mathfrak{A}(z)}=\frac{5+4z+8z^{2}+4z^{3}+5z^{4}+z^{5}}{1-z^{6}}\bmod 9\mathbb{Z}.

Note that

(48)

\frac{5+4z+8z^{2}+4z^{3}+5z^{4}+z^{5}}{1-z^{6}}=\frac{2z-1}{z^{2}-z+1}+\frac{3}{2(z+1)}\bmod 9\mathbb{Z}.

Let
s
be the roots of
z^{2}-z+1
in
\mathbb{C}_{3}
. We have
s\subset D_{\alpha_{2}}
because
(z^{2}-z+1)=(z+1)^{2}\bmod 3
. So,
D_{\alpha_{2}}
is a pole of
\mathfrak{A}^{\prime}(z)/\mathfrak{A}(z)
.

We now put

\epsilon_{1}=res\left(\frac{\mathfrak{h}^{\prime}(z)}{\mathfrak{h}(z)},D_{\alpha_{1}}\right)\text{ and }\epsilon_{2}=res\left(\frac{\mathfrak{A}^{\prime}(z)}{\mathfrak{A}(z)},D_{\alpha_{2}}\right).

Third Step
We now prove that
\epsilon_{1}\bmod 9=4
and that
\epsilon_{2}\bmod 9=8
. According to Third Step of the proof of Theorem
2.4
,
\epsilon_{1}\bmod 9=4
.

Now, regarding
\epsilon_{2}
, we have
\sum_{\tau\in\ s}\left(\frac{2z-1}{z^{2}-z+1},\tau\right)=2.
Thus, by (1) of Remark
6.0.1
and (ii) of Lemma
6.0.1
, we deduce from Equation (
48
) that

\epsilon_{2}\bmod 9=(2+3/2)\bmod 9=8.

Fourth Step
We now proceed to see that the conditions (1)-(2) of Theorem
6.1
are fulfilled. It follows from First Step and Second Step that
\textbf{Pol}(\mathfrak{h}^{\prime}(z)/\mathfrak{h}(z))=\{D_{\alpha_{1}}\}
and
\textbf{Pol}(\mathfrak{A}^{\prime}(z)/\mathfrak{A}(z))=\{D_{\alpha_{2}}\}
. In addition,
D_{\alpha_{1}}\neq D_{\alpha_{2}}
because
|\alpha_{1}-\alpha_{2}|=1
.

Further, we have seen in the proof of Theorem
2.4
that
\mathcal{L}_{1}(\mathfrak{h})=0
,
\alpha_{1}
is regular singular point of
\mathcal{L}_{1\mid\mathscr{M}^{2}}
and that the indicial polynomial of
\mathcal{L}_{1\mid\mathscr{M}^{2}}
at
\alpha_{1}
is
P=X(X-1)(X-2)+\frac{3}{2}X(X-1)\in\frac{\mathbb{Z}}{9\mathbb{Z}}[X]
. Now
\mathcal{H}(\mathfrak{A})=0
. We are going to see that
\alpha_{2}
is a regular singular point of
\mathcal{H}_{\mid\mathscr{M}^{2}}
. We first observe that
(1-34z+z^{2})=(1+z)^{2}\bmod 9\mathbb{Z}[[z]]
and that
3-153z+6z^{2}=(1+z)(3+6z)\bmod 9\mathbb{Z}[[z]]
. Whence,

(z+1)\frac{3-153z+6z^{2}}{z(1-34z+z^{2})}=\frac{3+6z}{z}\bmod\mathscr{M}^{2}\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{C}_{p}(z)},

(1+z)^{2}\frac{1-112z+7z^{2}}{z^{2}(1-34z+z^{2})}=\frac{1+5z+7z^{2}}{z^{2}}\bmod\mathscr{M}^{2}\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{C}_{p}(z)},

(z+1)^{3}\frac{z-5}{z^{2}(1-34z+z^{2})}=\frac{(z+1)(z-5)}{z^{2}}\bmod\mathscr{M}^{2}\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{C}_{p}(z)}.

Further, it is clear that
\frac{3+6z}{z}
,
\frac{1+5z+7z^{2}}{z^{2}}
and that
\frac{(z+1)(z-5)}{z^{2}}
belong to
\mathbb{Z}_{3}[[z+1]]
. So
\alpha_{2}
is a regular singular point of
\mathcal{H}_{\mid\mathscr{M}^{2}}
. In addition, the indicial polynomial of
\mathcal{H}_{\mid\mathscr{M}^{2}}
at
\alpha_{2}
is

R=X(X-1)(X-2)+3X(X-1)+3X=X(X^{2}+2)\in\frac{\mathbb{Z}}{9\mathbb{Z}}[[z]].

So, the conditions (1)-(2) of Theorem
6.1
are satisfied and, in oder to apply it, we suppose, towards a contradiction, that
\mathfrak{h}(z)
and
\mathfrak{A}(z)
are algebraically dependent over
E_{K}
and thus we have two cases

case A:

res\left(\frac{\mathfrak{h}^{\prime}(z)}{\mathfrak{h}(z)},D_{\alpha_{1}}\right)
belongs to
\mathbb{Z}_{3}
and its reduction modulo
p^{2}
is an exponent of
\mathcal{L}_{1\mid\mathscr{M}^{2}}
at
\alpha_{1}
.

Or

case B:
res\left(\frac{\mathfrak{A}^{\prime}(z)}{\mathfrak{A}(z)},D_{\alpha_{2}}\right)
belongs to
\mathbb{Z}_{3}
and its reduction modulo
p^{2}
is an exponent of
\mathcal{H}_{1\mid\mathscr{M}^{2}}
at
\alpha_{2}
.

Finally, let us see that cases A and B lead to a contradiction. From Third Step, we have

res\left(\frac{\mathfrak{h}^{\prime}(z)}{\mathfrak{h}(z)},D_{\alpha_{1}}\right)\bmod 9=\lambda_{1}\bmod 9=4.

So case A implies that
4
is a solution of
P
. That is a contradiction because
P(4)=6
. Again, by Third Step,

res\left(\frac{\mathfrak{A}^{\prime}(z)}{\mathfrak{A}(z)},D_{\beta_{1}}\right)\bmod 9=\lambda_{2}\bmod 9=8.

Therefore,
8
is a solution of
R
. Again, that is a contradiction because
R(8)=6
.

(ii) Suppose that
\mathfrak{f}(z)
and
\mathfrak{A}(z)
are algebraically dependent over
E_{K}
. Then, there are integers
a,b
not all zero such that
\mathfrak{f}^{a}(z)\mathfrak{A}^{b}(z)\in E_{K}.
According to (i) of Theorem
2.4
,
\mathfrak{f}/\mathfrak{h}\in E_{K}
. Thus,

\mathfrak{h}^{a}(z)\mathfrak{A}^{b}(z)=\frac{\mathfrak{h}^{a}(z)}{\mathfrak{f}^{a}(z)}\mathfrak{f}^{a}(z)\mathfrak{A}^{b}(z)\in E_{K}.

Whence,
\mathfrak{h}(z)
and
\mathfrak{A}(z)
are algebraically dependent over
E_{K}
.That contradicts (ii). Therefore,
\mathfrak{f}(z)
and
\mathfrak{A}(z)
are algebraically independent over
E_{K}
. Consequently, Theorem
2.3
implies that, for all integers
r,s\geq 0
,
\mathfrak{f}^{(r)}(z)
and
\mathfrak{A}^{(s)}(z)
are algebraically independent over
E_{K}
.

6.4.
Proof of Theorem
2.4

In order to prove Theorem
2.4
, we need the following lemma.

{lemm}

Let
p
be an odd prime,
\pi_{p}
be in
\overline{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
such that
\pi_{p}^{p-1}=-p
and let
N>0
be an integer.

(i)

For an integer
n\geq 1
, let
n=n_{0}+n_{1}p+\cdots+n_{l}p^{l}
be its
p
-adic expansion and let
\Sigma_{n}=n_{0}+n_{1}+\cdots+n_{l}
. Then

(49)

\left|\frac{\pi_{p}^{2n}}{4^{n}(n!)^{2}}\right|_{p}=(1/p)^{\frac{2\Sigma_{n}}{p-1}}.

(ii)

For every
1<s\leq N
,

(50)

\left|\binom{N}{s}f_{1}(z)^{s}\right|_{\mathcal{G}}<\left|Nf_{1}(z)\right|_{\mathcal{G}},

where
f_{1}(z)=\sum_{n\geq 1}\frac{\pi_{p}^{2n}}{4^{n}(n!)^{2}}z^{2n}.

Proof 6.10
.

(i) It is well-known that, for every integer
n\geq 1
,
|n!|_{p}=(1/p)^{(n-\Sigma_{n})/(p-1)}
. Whence, we immediately obtain the equality (
49
) for every integer
n\geq 1
.

(ii) It follows from (i) that
|f_{1}(z)|_{\mathcal{G}}=(1/p)^{2/p-1}
. Thus, the inequality (
50
) is equivalent to proving that, for every
1<s\leq N
,

(51)

\left|\binom{N}{s}\right|_{p}<\left|N\right|_{p}p^{\frac{2(s-1)}{p-1}}.

Let us suppose that
p
does not divide
N
. Then
|N|_{p}=1
and since
s>1
, we have
p^{2(s-1)/(p-1)}>1
. Further, the
p
-adic norm of
\binom{N}{s}
is less than or equal to 1 given that
\binom{N}{s}
is an integer. Hence, the inequality (
51
) holds.

Let us now suppose that
p
divides
N
and let
r
be the
p
-adic valuation of
N
. Then, we have
\left|N\right|_{p}p^{2(s-1)/(p-1)}=p^{\frac{2(s-1)}{p-1}-r}
. If
\frac{2(s-1)}{p-1}>r
then it is clear that the inequality (
51
) is true. Now, we suppose that
\frac{1}{p-1}\leq\frac{2(s-1)}{p-1}\leq r
. We first observe that
(N-1)(N-2)(N-3)(N-4)\cdots(N-s+1)=(-1)^{s-1}(s-1)!+Nt
for some integer
t>0
. Whence,

(N-1)(N-2)(N-3)(N-4)\cdots(N-s+1)=(-1)^{s-1}p^{\frac{s-1-\Sigma_{s-1}}{p-1}}a+p^{r}bt,

where
a,b
are integers not divisible by
p
. Since
\frac{1}{p-1}\leq\frac{2(s-1)}{p-1}\leq r
and
s>1
, we have
\frac{1}{p-1}\leq\frac{s-1}{p-1}<\frac{2(s-1)}{p-1}\leq r
. In addition, it is clear that
s-1-\Sigma_{s-1}<s-1
because
s>1
. So
\frac{s-1-\Sigma_{s-1}}{p-1}<\frac{s-1}{p-1}<r
. Thus

|(N-1)(N-2)(N-3)(N-4)\cdots(N-s+1)|_{p}=(1/p)^{\frac{s-1-\Sigma_{s-1}}{p-1}}.

Consequently,

|N(N-1)(N-2)\cdots(N-s+1)|_{p}=(1/p)^{r+\frac{s-1-\Sigma_{s-1}}{p-1}}.

So, by using the fact that
v_{p}(s!)=(s-\Sigma_{s})/(p-1)
, we get

\left|\binom{N}{s}\right|_{p}=\left(\frac{1}{p}\right)^{r+\frac{\Sigma_{s}-\Sigma_{s-1}-1}{p-1}}.

It is not hard to see that, for every
s\geq 2
,
s-\Sigma_{s-1}>1
and that
\Sigma_{s}\geq 1
. Thus,

\frac{\Sigma_{s}-\Sigma_{s-1}-1}{p-1}+\frac{s-1}{p-1}=\frac{s-\Sigma_{s-1}-1+\Sigma_{s}-1}{p-1}>0.

So

\frac{\Sigma_{s}-\Sigma_{s-1}-1}{p-1}>-\frac{s-1}{p-1}>\frac{-2(s-1)}{p-1}.

Whence,

r+\frac{\Sigma_{s}-\Sigma_{s-1}-1}{p-1}>r-\frac{2(s-1)}{p-1}.

Notice that
0\leq r-2(s-1)/(p-1)
because we have assumed that
2(s-1)/(p-1)\leq r
. Then

\left|\binom{N}{s}\right|_{p}=(1/p)^{r+\frac{\Sigma_{s}-\Sigma_{s-1}-1}{p-1}}\leq(1/p)^{r-\frac{2(s-1)}{p-1}}=|N|_{p}p^{\frac{2(s-1)}{p-1}}.

That completes the proof of the inequality (
51
).

Proof 6.11

(Proof of Theorem
2.4
)
.

(i) We are going to see that
\exp(\pi_{p}z)^{p}
belongs to
E_{p}
. We have

\exp(\pi_{p}z)^{p}=\exp(p\pi_{p}z)=\sum_{n\geq 0}\frac{p^{n}\pi_{p}^{n}}{n!}z^{n}.

It follows from (i) of Lemma
6.4
that

\left|\frac{p^{n}\pi_{p}^{n}}{n!}\right|_{p}=(1/p)^{n+\frac{\Sigma_{n}}{p-1}}.

So
\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\left|\frac{p^{n}\pi_{p}^{n}}{n!}\right|_{p}=0
. Consequently,
\exp(\pi_{p}z)^{p}
belongs to
E_{K}
.

(ii) We put
\mathfrak{B}(z)=J_{0}(\pi_{p}z)
. We first show that
\mathfrak{B}(z)
is transcendental over
E_{p}
. For this purpose, aiming for a contradiction, suppose that
\mathfrak{B}(z)
is algebraic over
E_{p}
. Given that
J_{0}(z)
is solution of
\delta^{2}+z^{2}
then
\mathfrak{B}(z)
is solution of
\delta^{2}-\pi_{p}^{2}z^{2}
. It is clear that this differential operator is MOM at zero and given that by assumption
p\neq 2
then, according to Dwork
[
17
, §4]
, this differential operator has a strong Frobenius structure. Hence,
\mathfrak{B}(z)\in\mathcal{M}\mathcal{F}(K)
and, by Theorem
3
,
\mathfrak{B}^{\prime}(z)/\mathfrak{B}(z)
belongs to
E_{0,K}
. Since
E_{0,K}\subset E_{p}
and
\mathfrak{B}(z)
is algebraic over
E_{p}
, if follows from Theorem
3
that there exists an integer
N>0
such that
\mathfrak{B}(z)^{N}\in E_{p}
. It is clear that

\mathfrak{B}(z)=\sum_{n\geq 0}\frac{(-1)^{n}\pi_{p}^{2n}}{4^{n}(n!)^{2}}z^{2n}.

We set
f_{1}(z)=\sum_{n\geq 1}\frac{(-1)^{n}\pi_{p}^{2n}}{4^{n}(n!)^{2}}z^{2n}.
Then
\mathfrak{B}(z)^{N}=(1+f_{1}(z))^{N}=\sum_{s=0}^{N}\binom{N}{s}f_{1}(z)^{s}.

According to (ii) of Lemma
6.4
we know that, for every
1<s\leq N
,

\left|\binom{N}{s}f_{1}(z)^{s}\right|_{\mathcal{G}}<\left|Nf_{1}(z)\right|_{\mathcal{G}}.

From (i) of Lemma
6.4
, we deduce that
|f_{1}(z)|_{\mathcal{G}}=(1/p)^{2/p-1}
. Thus, for all
1<s\leq N
,

(52)

\left|\binom{N}{s}f_{1}(z)^{s}\right|_{\mathcal{G}}<(1/p)^{v_{p}(N)+\frac{2}{p-1}}.

By definition,
\pi_{p}
is solution of the Eisenstein polynomial
X^{p-1}+p
and thus,
\pi_{p}
is a uniformizer of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}(\pi_{p})
. Given that, for any
s\geq 1
,
\binom{N}{s}f_{1}(z)^{s}\in\mathbb{Q}_{p}(\pi_{p})[[z]]
so Inequality (
52
) implies that, for every
1<s\leq N
,
\binom{N}{s}f_{1}(z)^{s}\in(\pi_{p})^{r(p-1)+3}\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}(\pi_{p})}[[z]]
. Consequently, for every
1<s\leq N
,

\binom{N}{s}f_{1}(z)^{s}=0\bmod\pi_{p}^{(p-1)v_{p}(N)+3}\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}(\pi_{p})}[[z]].

Therefore, we obtain

(53)

\mathfrak{B}(z)^{N}=1+Nf_{1}(z)\bmod\pi_{p}^{(p-1)v_{p}(N)+3}\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}(\pi_{p})}[[z]].

Again, from (i) of Lemma
6.4
, we conclude that, for all
n\geq 0
,

(54)

\left|\frac{\pi_{p}^{2p^{n}}}{4^{p^{n}}(p^{n})!^{2}}\right|_{p}=(1/p)^{2/p-1}.

Consequently, for all
n\geq 0
,
N\frac{\pi_{p}^{2p^{n}}}{4^{p^{n}}(p^{n})!^{2}}\notin\pi_{p}^{(p-1)v_{p}(N)+3}\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}(\pi_{p})}
.

Now, it is clear that if
m\notin\{p^{n}\}_{n\geq 0}
then
\Sigma_{m}>1
and thus, by using (i) of Lemma
6.4
again, we obtain

\left|\frac{\pi_{p}^{2m}}{4^{m}(m)!^{2}}\right|_{p}=(1/p)^{\frac{2\Sigma_{m}}{p-1}}<(1/p)^{\frac{2}{p-1}}.

In particular, if
m\notin\{p^{n}\}_{n\geq 0}
then
N\frac{\pi_{p}^{2m}}{4^{m}(m)!^{2}}\in\pi_{p}^{(p-1)v_{p}(N)+3}\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}(\pi_{p})}
. Hence, we get

(55)

1+Nf_{1}(z)=1-N\sum_{n\geq 1}\frac{\pi_{p}^{2p^{n}}}{4^{p^{n}}(p^{n})!^{2}}z^{2p^{n}}\bmod\pi_{p}^{(p-1)v_{p}(N)+3}\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}(\pi_{p})}[[z]].

Since
\mathfrak{B}(z)^{N}
belongs to
E_{p}
and
\mathfrak{B}(z)^{N}\in\mathbb{Q}_{p}(\pi_{p})[[z]]
there are a rational function
R(z)\in\mathbb{Q}_{p}(\pi_{p})(z)
such that

\mathfrak{B}(z)^{N}=R(z)\bmod\pi_{p}^{(p-1)v_{p}(N)+3}\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}(\pi_{p})}[[z]].

Let us write
R(z)=\sum_{n\geq-M}a_{n}z^{n}
with
M\in\mathbb{N}
. Then, it follows from the previous equality and Equations (
53
) and (
55
) that

(56)

\sum_{n\geq-M}a_{n}z^{n}=1-N\sum_{n\geq 1}\frac{\pi_{p}^{2p^{n}}}{4^{p^{n}}(p^{n})!^{2}}z^{2p^{n}}\bmod\pi_{p}^{(p-1)v_{p}(N)+3}\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}(\pi_{p})}[[z]].

On the one hand, since
R(z)
is a rational function, the sequence
\{a_{n}\}_{n\geq 0}
is almost periodic. That is, for any
\epsilon>0
, there are an integer
n_{\epsilon}
such that, for all
n\geq n_{\epsilon}
,
|a_{n+n_{\epsilon}}-a_{n}|<\epsilon
. On the other hand, it follows from Equation (
56
) that, for all
m\notin\{2p^{k}:k\geq 1\}
,
a_{m}\in\pi_{p}^{(p-1)v_{p}(N)+3}\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}(\pi_{p})}
and thus,
|a_{m}|\leq(1/p)^{v_{p}(N)+\frac{3}{p-1}}
. Now, if we take
\epsilon^{\prime}=(1/p)^{v_{p}(N)+\frac{3}{p-1}}
there an integer
n_{\epsilon^{\prime}}
such that, for all
n\geq n_{\epsilon^{\prime}}
,
|a_{n+n_{\epsilon^{\prime}}}-a_{n}|<\epsilon^{\prime}
. In addition, it is clear that exists
n_{0}\in\{2p^{k}:k\geq 1\}
such that
n_{0}\geq n_{\epsilon^{\prime}}
and
n_{0}+n_{\epsilon^{\prime}}\notin\{2p^{k}:k\geq 1\}
. If
n_{0}=2p^{k_{0}}
, it follows from (
56
) that

a_{n_{0}}=\lambda\bmod\pi_{p}^{(p-1)v_{p}(N)+3}\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}(\pi_{p})}\text{ with }\lambda=-N\frac{\pi_{p}^{2p^{k_{0}}}}{4^{p^{k_{0}}}(p^{k_{0}})!^{2}}.

Thus
|a_{n_{0}}-\lambda|\leq\epsilon^{\prime}
. Further, from Equation (
54
), we have
|\lambda|=(1/p)^{v_{p}(N)+\frac{2}{p-1}}>\epsilon^{\prime}
. Since
|a_{n_{0}}|=|a_{n_{0}}-\lambda+\lambda|
and
|a_{n_{0}}-\lambda|\leq\epsilon^{\prime}<|\lambda|
, and since moreover the norm is non-Archimedean, we get that
|a_{n_{0}}-\lambda+\lambda|=|\lambda|
and hence,
|a_{n_{0}}|=|\lambda|
. Given that
n_{0}+n_{\epsilon^{\prime}}\notin\{2p^{k}:k\geq 1\}
we know that
|a_{n_{0}+n_{\epsilon^{\prime}}}|\leq\epsilon^{\prime}
. Therefore,
|a_{n_{0}}|>|a_{n_{0}+n_{\epsilon^{\prime}}}|
. Again, given that the norm is non-Archimedean, we get that
|a_{n_{0}+n_{\epsilon^{\prime}}}-a_{n_{0}}|=|a_{n_{0}}|=|\lambda|>\epsilon^{\prime}
. But, that is a contradiction because
|a_{n_{0}+n_{\epsilon^{\prime}}}-a_{n_{0}}|<\epsilon^{\prime}
. So
\mathfrak{B}(z)
is transcendental over
E_{p}
and since
E_{K}\subset E_{p}
,
\mathfrak{B}(z)
is transcendental over
E_{K}
.

Now, by Theorem
2.3
, we get that, for all
s\geq 0
,
\mathfrak{B}^{(s)}(z)
is transcendental over
E_{K}
. Finally, for all
s\geq 0
,
\mathfrak{B}^{(s)}(z)
is transcendental over
E_{K}[\exp(\pi_{p}z)]
because
\mathfrak{B}^{(s)}(z)
is transcendental over
E_{K}
and, according to (i),
\exp(\pi_{p}z)
is algebraic over
E_{K}
.

(iii) We put
\mathfrak{B}_{1}(z)=J_{0}(2i\sqrt{2}\pi_{p}z)
and
\mathfrak{B}_{2}(z)=\mathfrak{y}(\pi_{p}z)
. We first show that
\mathfrak{B}_{2}(z)
is transcendental over are
E_{K}
. It turns out that
\mathfrak{y}(z)=\exp(3z)J_{0}(2i\sqrt{2}z)
. So
\mathfrak{B}_{2}(z)=\exp(3\pi_{p}z)\mathfrak{B}_{1}(z)
and

\frac{\mathfrak{B}_{2}^{\prime}(z)}{\mathfrak{B}_{2}(z)}=\frac{3\pi_{p}\exp(3\pi_{p}z)}{\exp(3\pi_{p}z)}+\frac{2i\sqrt{2}\mathfrak{B}^{\prime}(2i\sqrt{2}z)}{\mathfrak{B}(2i\sqrt{2}z)}=3\pi_{p}+\frac{2i\sqrt{2}\mathfrak{B}^{\prime}(2i\sqrt{2}z)}{\mathfrak{B}(2i\sqrt{2}z)}=3\pi_{p}+\frac{\mathfrak{B}_{1}^{\prime}(z)}{\mathfrak{B}_{1}(z)}.

As we have already said at the beginning of (ii),
\mathfrak{B}(z)\in\mathcal{M}\mathcal{F}(K)
. Then, according to (iii) of Theorem
3
,
\mathfrak{B}^{\prime}(z)/\mathfrak{B}(z)\in E_{0,K}
. In particular,
\mathfrak{B}^{\prime}(z)/\mathfrak{B}(z)\in E_{0,p}
. Since
p\neq 2
, the
p
-adic norm of
2i\sqrt{2}
is 1. Thus,
\mathfrak{B}^{\prime}(2i\sqrt{2}z)/\mathfrak{B}(2i\sqrt{2}z)
is still an element of
E_{0,p}
. Whence,
\mathfrak{B}^{\prime}_{1}(z)/\mathfrak{B}_{1}(z)
belongs to
E_{0,p}
and hence,
\mathfrak{B}^{\prime}_{2}(z)/\mathfrak{B}_{2}(z)
is also in
E_{0,p}
. Suppose, towards a contradiction, that
\mathfrak{B}_{2}(z)
is transcendental over
E_{K}
. So, by Theorem
3
, we deduce that there there is a nonzero integer
a
such that
\mathfrak{B}_{2}(z)^{a}\in E_{p}
. That is,
\exp(3\pi_{p}z)^{a}\mathfrak{B}_{1}(z)^{a}\in E_{p}
. According to (i),
\exp(\pi_{p}z)^{p}\in E_{K}
and since
\exp(3\pi_{p}z)=\exp(\pi_{p}z)^{3}
, we have
\exp(\pi_{p}z)^{3p}\in E_{K}
. Consequently,
\mathfrak{B}_{1}(z)^{pa}\in E_{p}
. As the
p
-adic norm of
2i\sqrt{2}
is 1, we get
\mathfrak{B}(z)^{pa}\in E_{p}
. But, according to (ii),
\mathfrak{B}(z)
is transcendental over
E_{p}
and thus
a=0
. Which is a contradiction. So
\mathfrak{B}_{2}(z)
is transcendental over
E_{K}
. In addition, for all integers
s\geq 0
,
\mathfrak{B}^{(s)}_{2}(z)/\mathfrak{B}_{2}(z)\in E_{p}
because
\mathfrak{B}^{\prime}_{2}(z)/\mathfrak{B}_{2}(z)\in E_{p}
. Consequently, Theorem
3
and the fact that
\mathfrak{B}_{2}(z)
is transcendental over
E_{p}
imply that
\mathfrak{B}^{(s)}_{2}(z)
is transcendental over
E_{p}
. Finally, for all integers
s\geq 0
,
\mathfrak{B}^{(s)}_{2}(z)
is transcendental over the ring
E_{K}[\exp(\pi_{p}z)]
because, according to (i),
\exp(\pi_{p}z)
is algebraic over
E_{K}
and we have already seen that
\mathfrak{B}^{(s)}_{2}(z)
is transcendental over
E_{K}
.

6.5.
Proof of Theorem
2.4

Proof 6.12

(Proof of Theorem
2.4
)
.

Let
\mathfrak{B}(z)=J_{0}(\pi_{3}z)
and
K
be an finite extension of
\mathbb{Q}_{3}(\pi_{3})
such that
K
is a Frobenius field. At the beginning of the proof of Theorem
2.4
we proved that
\mathfrak{h}(z)\in\mathcal{M}\mathcal{F}(K)
and we have also seen at the beginning of proof of Theorem
2.4
that
\mathfrak{A}(z)\in\mathcal{M}\mathcal{F}(K)
. The fact that
\mathfrak{B}(z)\in\mathcal{M}\mathcal{F}(K)
has been shown at the beginning of (ii) of the proof of Theorem
2.4
.

(i) We have already seen in the proof of Theorems
2.4
and
2.4
that
\mathfrak{h}^{\prime}(z)/\mathfrak{h}(z)
belongs to
E(\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{C}_{3}}\setminus D_{1})
and that
\mathfrak{A}^{\prime}(z)/\mathfrak{A}(z)
belongs to
E(\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{C}_{3}}\setminus D_{-1})
. We have also shown there that
D_{1}
is the only pole of
\mathfrak{h}^{\prime}(z)/\mathfrak{h}(z)
and that
D_{-1}
is the only pole of
\mathfrak{A}^{\prime}(z)/\mathfrak{A}(z)
.

Notice that
\pi_{3}
is a uniformizer of
\mathbb{Q}_{3}(\pi_{3})
. As we already said
\mathfrak{B}(z)
is solution of the MOM differential operator
\mathcal{B}=\delta^{2}+\pi_{3}^{2}z
and, by
[
17
, §4]
, this differential operator has strong Frobenius structure with period
1
. So, according to Theorem
3
,
\mathfrak{B}(z)\in 1+\pi_{3}\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{Q}_{3}(\pi_{3})}[[z]]
,
T:=\mathfrak{B}(z)/\mathfrak{B}(z^{3})\in E_{0,\mathbb{Q}_{3}(\pi_{3})}
and
\mathfrak{B}^{\prime}(z)/\mathfrak{B}(z)\in E_{0,\mathbb{Q}_{3}(\pi_{3})}
. We are going to show that if
\alpha\in\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{C}_{3}}
then
D_{\alpha}
is not a pole of
\mathfrak{B}^{\prime}(z)/\mathfrak{B}(z)
. It follows from
[
17
, § 6]
that
T
converges in
D(0,1^{+})=\{x\in\mathbb{C}_{3}:|x|\leq 1\}
. Moreover, according to Remark
5.3
, we have

(57)

\frac{\mathfrak{B}^{\prime}(z)}{\mathfrak{B}(z)}\equiv\frac{T^{\prime}_{m}(z)}{T_{m}(z)}\bmod\pi_{3}^{m+1}\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{Q}_{3}(\pi_{3})}[[z]]\text{ with }T_{m}=T(z)T(z^{3})\cdots T(z^{3^{m}}).

Notice that
H\in 1+\pi_{3}\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{Q}_{3}(\pi_{3})}[[z]]
because
\mathfrak{B}(z)\in 1+\pi_{3}\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{Q}_{3}(\pi_{3})}[[z]]
. Thus, if
\alpha\in\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{C}_{3}}
,
|H(\alpha)|=1
and consequently, for all
m\geq 1
,
|H_{m}(\alpha)|=1
. So, from Equation (
57
),
D_{\alpha}
is not a pole of
\mathfrak{B}^{\prime}(z)/\mathfrak{B}(z)
.

Aiming for a contradiction, suppose that
\mathfrak{h}(z)
,
\mathfrak{A}(z)
, and
\mathfrak{B}(z)
are algebraically dependent over
E_{K}
. Then, according to Theorem
2.3
, there are integers
a
,
b
, and
c
not all zero such that
\mathfrak{h}(z)^{a}\mathfrak{A}(z)^{b}\mathfrak{B}(z)^{c}\in E_{0,K}
and, further, by Theorem
6.1
, we also have

(58)

\mathfrak{h}(z)^{a}\mathfrak{A}(z)^{b}\mathfrak{B}(z)^{c}=\prod_{i=1}^{2}(z-\beta_{i})^{\mu_{i}}\mathfrak{t}(z),

where
\mu_{i}=res\left(a\frac{\mathfrak{h}^{\prime}(z)}{\mathfrak{h}(z)}+b\frac{\mathfrak{A}^{\prime}(z)}{\mathfrak{A}(z)}+c\frac{\mathfrak{B}^{\prime}(z)}{\mathfrak{B}(z)},D_{\beta_{i}}\right)\in\mathbb{Z}
,
\beta_{1}\in D_{1}
and
\beta_{2}\in D_{-1}
, and
\mathfrak{t}(z)=\prod_{n=1}^{\infty}\left(\frac{z-a_{n}}{z-b_{n}}\right)
, where
(a_{n},b_{n})_{n\geq 1}
is a strongly copiercing sequence associated to
D_{0}
.

Notice that Remark
6
implies
D_{\beta_{1}}=D_{1}
and
D_{\beta_{2}}=D_{-1}
.

As
K
is a Frobenius then, by Theorem
2.4
, the power series
\mathfrak{h}(z)
and
\mathfrak{A}(z)
are algebraically independent over
E_{K}
and consequently,
c\neq 0
. Now, from Equation (
58
), we deduce that

(59)

c\frac{\mathfrak{B}^{\prime}(z)}{\mathfrak{B}(z)}=\sum_{i=1}^{2}\frac{\mu_{i}}{z-\beta_{i}}-a\frac{\mathfrak{h}^{\prime}(z)}{\mathfrak{h}(z)}-b\frac{\mathfrak{A}^{\prime}(z)}{\mathfrak{A}(z)}+\frac{\mathfrak{t}^{\prime}(z)}{\mathfrak{t}(z)}.

Now, we have the following factorization
\mathfrak{t}(z)=\prod_{i=1}^{4}\mathfrak{t}_{i}(z)
, where for
i\in\{1,2\}
,
\mathfrak{t}_{i}(z)=\prod_{b_{n}\in D_{\beta_{i}}}\left(\frac{z-a_{n}}{z-b_{n}}\right)
,
\mathfrak{t}_{3}(z)=\prod_{b_{n}\in\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{C}_{3}}\setminus D_{\beta_{1}}\cup D_{\beta_{2}}}\left(\frac{z-a_{n}}{z-b_{n}}\right)
and
\mathfrak{t}_{4}(z)=\prod_{b_{n}\notin\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{C}_{3}}}\left(\frac{z-a_{n}}{z-b_{n}}\right).
As the sequence
(a_{n},b_{n})_{n\geq 1}
is a strongly copiercing sequence associated to
D_{0}
then the sequences used to define
\mathfrak{t}_{1}(z),\ldots,\mathfrak{t}_{4}(z)
are also strongly copiercing sequence associated to
D_{0}
. Thus, by Remark
6.2
,
\mathfrak{t}_{1}(z),\ldots,\mathfrak{t}_{4}(z)
belong to
E_{p}
. Further, it follows from Equation (
59
) that

(60)

c\frac{\mathfrak{B}^{\prime}(z)}{\mathfrak{B}(z)}=\frac{\mu_{1}}{z-\beta_{1}}-a\frac{\mathfrak{h}^{\prime}(z)}{\mathfrak{h}(z)}+\frac{\mathfrak{t}^{\prime}_{1}(z)}{\mathfrak{t}_{1}(z)}+\frac{\mu_{2}}{z-\beta_{2}}-b\frac{\mathfrak{A}^{\prime}(z)}{\mathfrak{A}(z)}+\frac{\mathfrak{t}^{\prime}_{2}(z)}{\mathfrak{t}_{2}(z)}+\sum_{i=3}^{4}\frac{\mathfrak{t}^{\prime}_{i}(z)}{\mathfrak{t}_{i}(z)}.

On the one hand, by using Mittag-Leffet’s Theorem (see Equation (
27
)), we know that there exits a unique sequence
\{\alpha_{n}\}_{n\geq 1}
in
\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{C}_{3}}
such that, for every,
|\alpha_{n}|=1
,
|\alpha_{n}-\alpha_{m}|=1
for every
n\neq m
and there exists a unique sequence
\{f_{n}\}_{n\geq 0}
in
E_{0,K}
such that
f_{0}\in E(\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{C}_{3}})
, for
n>0
,
f_{n}\in E^{\alpha_{n}}
, and

c\frac{\mathfrak{B}^{\prime}(z)}{\mathfrak{B}(z)}=\sum_{n\geq 0}f_{n}.

But, we have already seen that for any
\alpha\in\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{C}_{3}}
,
D_{\alpha}
is not a pole of
\mathfrak{B}^{\prime}(z)/\mathfrak{B}(z)
and thus,
f_{n}=0
for all
n>0
.

On the other hand, it is clear
D_{\beta_{1}}
is the only of pole of
\frac{\mu_{1}}{z-\beta_{1}}-a\frac{\mathfrak{h}^{\prime}(z)}{\mathfrak{h}(z)}+\frac{\mathfrak{t}^{\prime}_{1}(z)}{\mathfrak{t}_{1}(z)}
and thus it belongs to
E^{\beta_{1}}
. Similarly,
D_{\beta_{2}}
is the only pole of
\frac{\mu_{2}}{z-\beta_{2}}-b\frac{\mathfrak{A}^{\prime}(z)}{\mathfrak{A}(z)}+\frac{\mathfrak{t}^{\prime}_{2}(z)}{\mathfrak{t}_{2}(z)}
and thus it belongs to
E^{\beta_{2}}
.

Since
f_{n}=0
for all
n>0
it follows from Equation (
60
) that

\frac{\mu_{1}}{z-\beta_{1}}-a\frac{\mathfrak{h}^{\prime}(z)}{\mathfrak{h}(z)}+\frac{\mathfrak{t}^{\prime}_{1}(z)}{\mathfrak{t}_{1}(z)}=0\text{ and }\frac{\mu_{2}}{z-\beta_{2}}-b\frac{\mathfrak{A}^{\prime}(z)}{\mathfrak{A}(z)}+\frac{\mathfrak{t}^{\prime}_{2}(z)}{\mathfrak{t}_{2}(z)}=0.

Given that
\frac{\mathfrak{t}^{\prime}_{1}(z)}{\mathfrak{t}_{1}(z)}=\sum_{b_{n}\in D_{\beta_{1}}}\left(\frac{1}{z-a_{n}}-\frac{1}{z-b_{n}}\right)
and
(a_{n},b_{n})_{b_{n}\in D_{\beta_{1}}}
is a strongly copiercing sequence associated to
D_{0}
, from Theorem 59.1 of
[
19
]
, we deduce that
\frac{\mu_{1}}{z-\beta_{1}}-a\frac{\mathfrak{h}^{\prime}(z)}{\mathfrak{h}(z)}
is integrable in
E_{0,p}
. Since
D_{\beta_{1}}
is the only pole of
\mathfrak{h}^{\prime}(z)/\mathfrak{h}(z)
and
\mu_{1}=res(a\mathfrak{h}^{\prime}(z)/\mathfrak{h}(z),D_{\beta_{1}})
, we get, from Lemma 59.2 of
[
19
]
, that
\frac{\mu_{1}}{z-1}-a\frac{\mathfrak{h}^{\prime}(z)}{\mathfrak{h}(z)}
is also integrable in
E_{0,p}
. Further, Theorem 59.11 of
[
19
]
implies that there exists an integer
e\geq 0
such that

\frac{\mu_{1}}{z-1}-a\frac{\mathfrak{h}^{\prime}(z)}{\mathfrak{h}(z)}=\frac{1}{p^{e}}\sum_{n\geq 1}\left(\frac{1}{z-c_{n}}-\frac{1}{z-d_{n}}\right),

where
(c_{n},d_{n})_{n\geq 1}
is a strongly copiercing sequence associated to
D_{0}
. So, by Remark
6.2
,
\mathfrak{j}(z)=\prod_{n=1}^{\infty}\left(\frac{z-c_{n}}{z-d_{n}}\right)
belongs to
E_{0,p}
. Thus, there exists
\lambda\in\mathbb{C}_{3}\setminus\{0\}
such that
\mathfrak{h}^{a}(z)=\lambda(z-1)^{\mu_{1}}\mathfrak{j}(z)^{1/p^{e}}
. Whence,
\mathfrak{h}^{ap^{e}}(z)=(z-1)^{\mu_{1}p^{e}}\mathfrak{j}_{1}(z)
, with
\mathfrak{j}_{1}(z)=\lambda^{p^{e}}\mathfrak{j}(z)
. We know that
\mathfrak{h}(z)\in 1+z\mathbb{Z}_{3}[[z]]
and thus
\mathfrak{h}(z)/(z-1)\in-1+z\mathbb{Z}_{3}[[z]]
. So, we conclude that
\mathfrak{j}_{1}(z)\in E_{0,\mathbb{Q}_{3}}

Similarly, we can prove that there exists an integer
m\geq 0
such that
\mathfrak{A}^{bp^{m}}(z)=(z+1)^{\mu_{2}p^{m}}\mathfrak{j}_{2}(z)
, with
\mathfrak{j}_{2}(z)\in E_{0,\mathbb{Q}_{3}}
.

Thus,
\mathfrak{h}^{ap^{e}}(z)\mathfrak{A}^{bp^{m}}(z)\in E_{0,\mathbb{Q}_{3}}
. But, we know that
\mathfrak{h}(z)
and
\mathfrak{A}(z)
are algebraically independent over
E_{\mathbb{Q}_{3}}
and therefore,
ap^{e}=0=bp^{m}
. Whence
a=0=b
. But, we know that
\mathfrak{h}(z)^{a}\mathfrak{A}(z)^{b}\mathfrak{B}(z)^{c}\in E_{0,K}
and thus
\mathfrak{B}(z)^{c}\in E_{0,K}
, which is a contradiction because
c\neq 0
and
\mathfrak{B}(z)
is transcendental over
E_{K}
.

Therefore,
\mathfrak{h}(z)
,
\mathfrak{A}(z)
, and
\mathfrak{B}(z)
are algebraically independent over
E_{K}
. So, from Theorem
2.3
we conclude that, for all integers
r,s,k\geq 0
,
\mathfrak{B}^{(r)}(z)
, the power series
\mathfrak{A}^{(s)}(z)
, and
\mathfrak{h}^{(k)}(z)
are algebraically independent over
E_{K}
.

(ii) Aiming for a contradiction, suppose that
\mathfrak{f}(z)
,
\mathfrak{A}(z)
and
\mathfrak{B}(z)
are algebraically dependent over
E_{K}
. At the beginning of the proof of Theorem
2.4
we showed that
\mathfrak{f}(z)\in\mathcal{M}\mathcal{F}(K)
and we have already seen that
\mathfrak{A}(z)
and
\mathfrak{B}(z)
belong to
\mathcal{M}\mathcal{F}(K)
. Thus, according to Theorem
2.3
, there are integers
a
,
b
, and
c
not all zero such that
\mathfrak{f}(z)^{a}\mathfrak{A}(z)^{b}\mathfrak{B}(z)^{c}\in E_{0,K}
. But, according to (i) of Theorem
2.4
,
\mathfrak{h}/\mathfrak{f}
belongs to
E_{\mathbb{Q}_{3}}
. Thus

\mathfrak{h}(z)^{a}\mathfrak{A}(z)^{b}\mathfrak{B}(z)^{c}=\left(\frac{\mathfrak{h}(z)}{\mathfrak{f}(z)}\right)^{a}\mathfrak{f}(z)^{a}\mathfrak{A}(z)^{b}\mathfrak{B}(z)^{c}\in E_{K}.

That is a contradiction because, according to (i),
\mathfrak{h}(z)
,
\mathfrak{A}(z)
and
\mathfrak{B}(z)
are algebraically independent over
E_{K}
. Therefore,
\mathfrak{f}(z)
,
\mathfrak{A}(z)
and
\mathfrak{B}(z)
are algebraically independent over
E_{K}
. Finally, according to Theorem
2.3
, for all integers
r
,
s
,
k\geq 0
,
\mathfrak{B}^{(r)}(z)
,
\mathfrak{A}^{(s)}(z)
, and
\mathfrak{f}^{(k)}(z)
are algebraically independent over
E_{K}
.
